│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │

│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was addressed by implementing two security-related changes in the `fastapi` repository:           │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     1.  **OpenAPI Server Configuration**: In `fastapi/applications.py`, the default value of the                │
│ `root_path_in_servers` parameter was changed from `True` to `False`. This prevents the `root_path` from being   │
│ automatically added to the `servers` list in the OpenAPI schema, mitigating potential exploitation if a proxy   │
│ is misconfigured to allow malicious `x-forwarded-*` headers.                                                    │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     2.  **Swagger UI Escaping**: In `fastapi/openapi/docs.py`, the `get_swagger_ui_html` function was updated   │
│ to use `json.dumps()` when inserting `openapi_url` and `oauth2_redirect_url` into the HTML/JavaScript template. │
│ This ensures that these values are properly escaped as JavaScript string literals, preventing potential XSS     │
│ vulnerabilities if these values are compromised.                                                                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     These changes were verified using a reproduction script that confirmed the new default value of             │
│ `root_path_in_servers` and the correct escaping of URLs in the Swagger UI HTML output.                          │

│ `example.com`). This behavior mirrored a bug in CPython's `urllib.request.proxy_bypass` (bpo-39057).            │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I have updated `should_bypass_proxies` in `src/requests/utils.py` to implement strict domain boundary       │
│ matching. A hostname now matches a `no_proxy` entry only if it is exactly equal to the entry (after removing    │
│ leading dots) or if it ends with `.` followed by the entry. Additionally, I implemented port-specific matching  │
│ to ensure that if a port is specified in `no_proxy`, it is correctly compared against the URL's port. To avoid  │
│ the underlying CPython bug, the function now returns its own result and skips the call to                       │
│ `urllib.request.proxy_bypass` whenever `no_proxy` is configured.                                                │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The bug was caused by logic in `src/requests/adapters.py` within the `request_url` method that explicitly   │
│ collapsed leading slashes (converting `//` to `/`) to avoid confusing `urllib3`. This behavior corrupted S3     │
│ presigned URLs, which may require multiple leading slashes in the path.                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The fix involved removing the following lines from `src/requests/adapters.py`:                              │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│             if url.startswith("//"):                                                                            │
│                 url = "/" + url[2:]                                                                             │
│     ```                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     This ensures that the original path URL is preserved and passed to the underlying connection, maintaining   │
│ the integrity of S3 presigned URLs.                                                                             │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `Response` objects were including themselves in their own `history` list during a        │
│ redirect chain. This occurred because `Session.resolve_redirects` was explicitly assigning `resp.history =      │
│ hist[1:]` to each intermediate response, where `hist` included the current response.                            │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I have fixed this by:                                                                                       │
│     1. Removing the `resp.history = hist[1:]` assignment in `src/requests/sessions.py`.                         │
│     2. Updating the redirect limit check from `len(resp.history) >= self.max_redirects` to `len(hist) - 1 >=    │
│ self.max_redirects` to ensure that the `max_redirects` limit is still correctly enforced based on the number of │
│ redirects encountered.                                                                                          │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The final response's history is correctly populated by `Session.send()` after the `resolve_redirects`       │
│ generator completes, so removing the intermediate assignment does not break the expected behavior for the final │
│ response.                                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Verification was performed using a reproduction script that simulated a redirect chain and checked for      │
│ self-referencing responses. The script now confirms that intermediate responses have empty histories and the    │
│ final response contains the correct history without referencing itself.                                         │

│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │

│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `_parse_content_type_header` in `src/requests/utils.py` incorrectly assigned a value of  │
│ `True` to parameters in the `Content-Type` header that lacked an equals sign (`=`). This behavior contradicted  │
│ RFC specifications (1521 to 9110) and caused a crash in `get_encoding_from_headers` when a `charset` parameter  │
│ without an `=` was present, as the code attempted to call `.strip()` on the boolean value `True`.               │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I modified `_parse_content_type_header` to skip value assignment for any parameter that does not contain an │
│ `=`, ensuring that only valid `key=value` pairs are added to the parameters dictionary. I also updated          │
│ `tests/test_utils.py` to remove the outdated expectations that malformed parameters should be assigned `True`.  │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Verification:                                                                                               │
│     - Updated `_parse_content_type_header` to ignore parameters without `=`.                                    │
│     - Updated `tests/test_utils.py` to reflect the correct behavior.                                            │
│     - Verified that `test__parse_content_type_header` passes.                                                   │
│     - Verified with a reproduction script that `get_encoding_from_headers` no longer crashes and correctly      │
│ falls back to the default encoding when a malformed `charset` parameter is present.                             │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `FileProxy` in `rich/file_proxy.py` inherited from `io.TextIOBase`, which provides a     │
│ default `isatty()` implementation that always returns `False`. Consequently, `FileProxy.__getattr__` was never  │
│ called for `isatty()`, and the proxy failed to report the actual TTY status of the wrapped file.                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     To fix this, I explicitly implemented the `isatty()` method in `FileProxy` to delegate the call to the      │
│ wrapped file, mirroring the existing implementation of `fileno()`.                                              │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│         def isatty(self) -> bool:                                                                               │
│             return self.__file.isatty()                                                                         │
│     ```                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     This ensures that `FileProxy` correctly proxies the `isatty()` status of the underlying file object. The    │
│ fix was verified using a reproduction script with a mock file.                                                  │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│     ... (1 earlier lines)                                                                                       │
│     The issue was that `console.print()` defaulted to `objects = (NewLine(),)` when no positional arguments     │
│ were provided. Since `NewLine()` always renders a newline character, the `end` parameter was effectively        │
│ ignored when `objects` was empty, leading to an unwanted newline even if `end=""` was specified.                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     By changing the default to `objects = ("",)`, the `_collect_renderables` method now creates a `Text` object │
│ with the specified `end` value. When this `Text` object is rendered, it correctly respects the `end` parameter, │
│ printing a newline only if `end` is not empty.                                                                  │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│     # rich/console.py                                                                                           │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     -        if not objects:                                                                                    │
│     -            objects = (NewLine(),)                                                                         │
│     +        if not objects:                                                                                    │
│     +            objects = ("",)                                                                                │
│     ```                                                                                                         │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│     ... (3 earlier lines)                                                                                       │
│     I have implemented a check in `APIRouter.include_router` to prevent this. The check ensures that a router   │
│ cannot be included in itself, either directly or if it's the `FastAPI` application that owns the router.        │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Specifically, I added the following check at the beginning of the `include_router` method in                │
│ `fastapi/routing.py`:                                                                                           │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│     if router is self or getattr(router, "router", None) is self:                                               │
│         raise FastAPIError("You cannot include a router into itself.")                                          │
│     ```                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     This prevents both `APIRouter.include_router(router)` and `FastAPI.include_router(app)` from causing an     │
│ infinite loop, raising a `FastAPIError` instead.                                                                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I verified the fix with a reproduction script that tested both scenarios, and both now correctly raise the  │
│ `FastAPIError`.                                                                                                 │

│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │

│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The bug was caused by the `on_startup` and `on_shutdown` attributes being initialized in                    │
│ `APIRouter.__init__` before the call to `super().__init__()`. Since `APIRouter` inherits from Starlette's       │
│ `Router`, the `super().__init__` call was overwriting these attributes, rendering the provided handlers         │
│ ineffective.                                                                                                    │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I have moved the initialization of `self.on_startup` and `self.on_shutdown` to occur after the              │
│ `super().__init__` call in `fastapi/routing.py`. This ensures that the handlers are correctly preserved and can │
│ be executed by the lifespan manager.                                                                            │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Verification was performed using a reproduction script that created an `APIRouter` with `on_startup` and    │
│ `on_shutdown` handlers and verified their execution during the application's lifespan. The fix was confirmed to │
│ resolve the issue.                                                                                              │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `fastapi/dependencies/utils.py` in the `analyze_param` function explicitly forbade the   │
│ use of `Depends` (and other FastAPI annotations) for several special types, including `Response`. This caused   │
│ an `AssertionError` when users tried to use `Response` as a dependency, for example via `Annotated[Response,    │
│ Depends(...)]`.                                                                                                 │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I modified the `analyze_param` function to bypass these assertions specifically when the type annotation is │
│ a subclass of `Response` and a `depends` object is provided. This allows `Response` to be used as a dependency  │
│ while maintaining the existing behavior for regular `Response` injection and preventing other special types     │
│ (like `Request`) from being used with `Depends`.                                                                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Verification was performed using a reproduction script that confirmed:                                      │
│     - `Annotated[Response, Depends(...)]` now works.                                                            │
│     - `Response = Depends(...)` now works.                                                                      │
│     - Regular `response: Response` injection still works.                                                       │
│     - Using `Depends` with `Request` still correctly raises an `AssertionError`.                                │
│     - Using other FastAPI annotations (like `Query`) with `Response` without `Depends` still correctly raises   │
│ an `AssertionError`.                                                                                            │
